# ON THE NATURE OF THINGS CHAPTER VII.

Christ rises from the dead and allows himself to be seen, but only his own people recognize him; he thus walks about in heavenly form, and sometimes also in his own, until the Feast of Pentecost <note>The Christian festival celebrating the descent of the Holy Spirit; here used as an alchemical metaphor for the final stage of the "Great Work."</note>. For here, the highest perfection is tested within him: whether he will now persist in angelic form and eat solely of the Divine Essence. And then the Holy Spirit comes and goes forth with his power out of the entire *corpus* <note>body</note>, out of both body and soul. This then *tinctures* <term>Tincture: In alchemy, a substance capable of transforming or "dyeing" base matter into a higher state, or healing the soul.</term> that which was dead and broken, as was seen on the day of Pentecost, when Saint Peter, with his heavenly *Mercurio* <note>Mercury; here referring to the spiritualized essence or "living water" of the alchemists</note>, tinctured 3000 souls at once and released them from death.

Dear Seekers, herein lies the little pearl. If you possessed the *universal* <note>The Universal Medicine or Philosopher's Stone</note>, you could also tincture as Saint Peter did; but your greedy death keeps you hindered and locked away. Because you seek greed and worldly honor in luxury, thereby birthing yourselves into the quality of the night, the pearl remains hidden from you. However, the day shall shine again when the grim wrath of God is fulfilled in the blood of the saints, softened, and becomes a life of love. And the time is near.

# ON THE SIGNATURE

God said to Adam and Eve after the Fall: "The seed of the woman shall crush the serpent's head." He did not say the seed of the man. Within this lies the <term>Baptism of Nature</term><note>The process by which the harsh qualities of nature are purified and reborn into a spiritual state.</note>. The male principle possesses the fire-spirit, and the female principle possesses the water-spirit for the <term>tincture</term><note>In alchemy, the "tincture" is the spiritual essence or "dye" that can transform and heal matter.</note>. However, Mercury is a fire-life, and it creates a body for itself according to its hunger and desire. Therefore, at the beginning, the goal is to give the fire-hunger a lovely Virgin from its own lineage as a companion, so that its fierce hunger may be transformed into love. Then they may sleep together in their eternal marriage bed.

The Devil is an enemy of marriage; he then approaches with joyful desire and attacks these spouses. He is not permitted to harm them with his hands, but he plagues them with a false, joyful desire. If they set their desire toward his will, and if his desire overcomes them, then the two become hateful toward one another and give birth to a false child. For Christ said: "A bad tree brings forth bad fruit, and a good tree, good fruit."

Therefore, the <term>Artist</term><note>The "Artist" refers to the Alchemist or the spiritual seeker performing the "Great Work."</note> should guard himself against such wrath, and yet prepare crosses for the two spouses—for he is both their enemy and their friend—so that the two, in their marriage bed and in their love, may raise their desire toward God. Thus, through their desire, they may draw God's essence into their desire...

### The Actual Process in the Figuration of the Magical Child.

The course of Christ on Earth is a true prototype / showing how the new Child in the mother's womb is nourished after conception (as mentioned above) / and receives a vegetable life <note>A "vegetable life" refers to basic growth and nutrition, similar to a plant, before the higher soul is awakened.</note> and grows up / until the time of the true life of the Soul and Spirit / and how the Child arises from the essence of the parents / and in the ignition of the true life / as its own life / casts away the parental vegetation and influence / and arises like its own growth / as a new individual effect / according to the quality of the now newly ignited Spirit / where the Child becomes nobler than its parents / understood according to their outward life.

Because the entire marriage stands within the Devil’s temptation, one must pay close attention to his intentions. Therefore, the artist <term>Künstler: the alchemist or practitioner</term> must take care that he does not allow a devil that is too fierce to tempt the matter, nor one that is too weak, lest the Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius"; here representing the volatile, transformative spirit of the metal</note> becomes frivolous and desires to remain in its own poisonous, deadly property, swallowing the Baptism <note>The purifying liquid or solvent</note> like a wolf and remaining his old self.

As soon as he sees the form of the angels, let him lead Christ out of the wilderness and let the Bridegroom eat his own food again. Dismiss the Devil so that he plagues him no longer; then Christ will perform many miracles and signs, at which the artist will marvel and rejoice. At that point, the artist has nothing left to do; the Bride is within the Bridegroom, they are already wedded. He only needs to prepare the bed for them; they will surely warm it themselves. The Bridegroom embraces the Bride, and the Bride the Bridegroom; this is their food and their pastime until they conceive a child. Or, if the artist wishes to be so diligent as to warm the bed for the two spouses, let him take great care not to anger them in their love. Whatever he begins, he must carry through, for the Bridegroom is singular and wondrous. He always—

It is a wonder in the eyes of reason that God maintained such a process with the restoration of humanity in Christ; that He revealed Himself in such a wretched and despised form in human nature, and allowed Himself to be mocked, scorned, scourged, crucified, and killed; He allowed Himself to be buried, and rose again from the grave, and walked upon the Earth for a full forty days after His resurrection before He entered into His invisible Kingdom. Reason is so blind that it understands nothing of the eternal birth: it knows nothing of Paradise, how Adam was in Paradise, how he fell, and what the curse of the Earth might be. If they understood this, then the entire process would be revealed to them—just as the eternal birth is within itself, so also is the process of restoration after the Fall, and so also is the process of the Sages with their <term>Philosopher's Stone<note>The "Lapis Philosophorum," a legendary substance in alchemy capable of turning base metals into gold and achieving spiritual perfection.</note></term>. The reader should now consider what was written previously concerning the Center of Birth <note>original: "Centro der Gebährung"; refers to the spiritual point from which all life originates</note> of all beings; if he does so, he can easily proceed here. Everything that is corporeal, whether it be a spirit or a body, exists in a Sulphuric property <note>In alchemy, "Sulphur" represents the active, combustible, and soul-like principle of matter</note>; the spirit in such a spiritual property, and the body—arising out of the spirit—in such a corporeal property. For all things have originated from the eternal Spirit as a likeness of the eternal. The invisible Being, which is God and Eternity, has introduced itself through its own Desire into a visible being, and revealed itself within Time. Thus, He exists within Time as a Life, and Time exists within Him as something mute—just as a master craftsman creates his work with his tool, and the tool is mute in the master's hand, yet it performs the making, for the master merely guides it. Therefore, all things are enclosed within target, measure, and weight <note>A biblical allusion to Wisdom 11:20, suggesting a divinely ordered universe</note> according to the eternal birth; they run their course in their activity and birth according to the Law and property of Eternity. # DESIGNATURA

These two kingdoms stand within one another in constant strife, and there is a steady stirring within them, like boiling water near a fire. Each seethes in its own property: the <term>Glitetische</term><note>A term likely derived from the Middle High German 'glit' or 'gleit,' referring to a smooth, slippery, or shining quality; here used to describe the radiant, heavenly, or 'gliding' nature of light.</note> in joy and gentleness, and the deadly one in the anguish of darkness. Yet, one is the cause of the other. The light is the death, dying, and destruction of the darkness and its anguish; for in the light, the anguish has no power, but is rather <term>transmuted</term><note>original: "transmutiret"; an alchemical term for the transformation of one substance into another, higher form.</note> into the exaltation of the kingdom of joy, becoming the very cause of that joy. Otherwise, there would be no joy, for gentleness is a stillness, but the <term>torment</term><note>original: "Quall"; refers to the active, churning source of energy or 'quality' that drives life.</note> of anguish makes it rise up, like a laugh.

Thus, the anguish—as darkness—is the death and destruction of the radiant property. For if it gains dominion within the radiant property, it takes possession of the body and turns the oil into a poisonous torment, like a dark spirit or a body entirely earthly. This is what happened to <term>Adam</term><note>Referring to the biblical first man and his 'Fall' from a spiritual state into a material, mortal one.</note> when he imagined into evil. Yet, we must not understand this to mean that the radiant property itself takes on a poisonous torment; rather, <term>Mercury</term><note>original: "Mercurius"; in Böhmean alchemy, this represents the 'fever' or the active, moving life-force and word.</note> as the fire-life leads itself into anguish and poisons the essence of time—which is what the external Mercury does in its own desire. That is to say: it departs from the inner radiant essence, so that the inner remains standing immovable within itself, and the essence and spirit of time separate from the essence and spirit of eternity, and is—

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER VIII.

Yet there is no separation, but rather two Principles <note>original: "Principia"; referring to the fundamental spiritual and material forces</note> remain in one being, though there are indeed two beings; but one does not grasp the other, just as time does not grasp eternity. For in this way Adam and Eve also died: the Soul-Mercury <note>The "Mercurius" here represents the active, transformative spirit or life-force within the soul</note> departed from the being of eternity with its imagination and entered into the being of time, as if into the torment of anguish. Thus, the being of eternity lost its leader. But Christ brought this leader back again with the Divine Word, or Mercury. In this way, the being of eternity—which had been abandoned by the Soul-Mercury in Adam—regained *Life*.

And so we know that within the anguished Mercury, the being of eternity lies hidden as if in the enclosure of Death. This is the purpose of our writing and teaching: how one might bring the poisonous Mercury, with its desire, to the point where it enters back into the being of eternity (as if into that which is locked away), and takes up the being of eternity as a body once more. By doing so, it may tincture <note>term: "tingire" (to tincture); in alchemy, this means to permeate and transform a substance into a higher state</note> the being of time and bring them into one, so that the entire Body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> of the inner and outer world may be only one; that there may be only one Will, one Spirit, like a hunger for Love. Then that same hunger creates for itself only one kind of being, so that every spirit eats of its own being or body, and thereafter no evil will can ever enter into it again.

# ON THE SIGNATURE

Thus we understand that joy and sorrow, love and enmity, all originate through imagination and Desire <note>original: "Lust" — in this context, a creative or divine longing rather than mere carnal desire</note>; for in the Desire toward God, as toward free Love, the kingdom of joy arises in the midst of the anguish of death. And if the desire goes out from free Love into the anguish of death, as into the torment of darkness, then the desire is filled with the torment of death; thus also does Mercury <term>Mercury: the vital, moving spirit or "workmaster" in both alchemy and the human soul</term> operate within the torment of death.

Therefore we say with good reason: That no thing is so evil that a good does not lie within it, but the malice is not capable of receiving the good. Likewise, in the most poisonous Mercury lies the greatest Pearl-Jewel; if its poisonous will can be led into that [Pearl], then it reveals the Pearl itself, for it transmutes itself. This is to be seen in the Earth, where Mercury seeks its Pearl and turns it into gold within the coarse stone, or into other metals, all according to how the Sulfur <term>Sulfur: the formative, fiery principle that gives substance and "body" to the alchemical process</term> is in each place. For there is a constant wrestling in the Earth; Eternity longs to be rid of the time of Vanity <note>original: "Eitelkeit" — referring to the fleeting, empty nature of the material world</note>, and in its longing it yields itself to Mercury as its life and workmaster. And when Mercury receives this into its hunger, it becomes joyful and makes that same Free-Desire bodily within itself; therein is Gold and Silver, alongside other metals.

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER VIII.

Metals and good herbs; everything according to the power of each place. Just as the seething <note>original: "Sud" - refers to the internal heat or "cooking" process of the earth</note> is in each place, so too is the metal, all according to the property of the seven forms of Nature. Whichever form is primary in a location, according to that property a metal grows, as do herbs and trees.

In this, it is for the physician <note>original: "Medicus"</note> to note that they must learn to recognize the distinction of which property is the strongest in every thing with which they wish to cure. If they do not know this, they will often give death to their patients. But if he can receive his own Salt according to his property (for which he hungers), then he rejoices and lets his poisonous torment sink into the fire of Mars <note>Refers to the bitter, stinging quality of the planetary spirit Mars</note>.

God gives power to all life, whether it be evil or good, to each according to its own desire; for He is Himself all things, and yet He is not called "God" according to every essence, but rather according to the Light. In that Light He dwells within Himself and shines with power through all His essences. He imparts His power to all His essences and works, and every thing receives His power according to its own property: one receives Darkness, the other Light. Every hunger desires its own property, and yet the whole essence is entirely God’s, whether it be evil or good. For from and through Him is everything; that which is not of His Love is of His Wrath. <note>The author uses "Wrath" to describe the harsh, contracting, or "dark" quality of the divine fire that exists apart from the "Light" of Love.</note>

Paradise is still in the world, but man is not within it, unless he be born again of God; then, according to that same new rebirth, he is within it, and not with the Adam of the four elements. <note>The "four-elemental Adam" refers to the physical, mortal body composed of earth, air, fire, and water, which cannot perceive the spiritual realm.</note> If only we would allow ourselves to be taught to know ourselves, we would surely understand it by looking at created beings.

In the same way, God also dwells in all things, and yet the thing knows nothing of God; He is not manifest to the thing, and yet it receives power from Him. However, it takes that power from Him according to its own <term>Property<note>German: *Eigenschafft*. In Boehme's thought, this refers to the inherent quality or "signature" of a being.</note></term>, either from His Love or from His <term>Wrath<note>German: *Grimmen*. Yet the good is also within it, but entirely locked away, as it were, by the malice. You have an example of this in a thorn-bush and in other prickly things, out of which a beautiful, sweet-smelling flower nevertheless grows. See how two properties lie therein: one physical and one hostile. Whichever one triumphs, that one shapes the fruit.

It is the same with Man: he was created as a beautiful Paradisiacal flower and fruit. I know and see clearly that the mocker, cloaked in the Devil’s cover, will make me even more suspect because I write of an inner and an outer Mercury. By the **inner**, I mean the Word of God, or the **Divine Resonance** <note>original: "Göttlichen Hall"; refers to the sounding or echoing of the creative Word</note>, as the revelation of the eternity of the **Abyss** <note>original: "vngrundes"; a central concept in Boehme's thought referring to the "groundless" or "unfathomable" origin of God before creation</note>. By the **outer**, I mean the **Steward in Nature** <note>original: "Amptman in der Natur"</note>, acting as the instrument which the inner, living, and powerful Word—the Divine Resonance—guides, so that he may create and work.

Therefore, the <term>Sophist</term> <note>A term used here to describe a deceptive or superficial scholar who argues for the sake of winning rather than truth</note> will misinterpret me and say: "I am mixing them into one, and I hold Nature to be God," just as <term>Babel</term> <note>A symbolic name for the confused and divided state of the institutional church</note> has done to me before. To him I say: let him look at my words rightly and learn to understand them correctly. For I speak at times from the **heavenly Mercury**, looking at that alone, and then immediately after at the instrument of the heavenly. Therefore, let him pay attention to the meaning: I do not write in a purely heavenly manner, but **Theosophically** <note>original: "Theosophisch"; meaning a wisdom derived from direct divine insight rather than mere human logic</note>, from a higher ground than the outer **Master-Builder** <note>original: "Werckmeister"</note> is, and then also from that same builder.

### CHAPTER IX.

#### Concerning the Signature, how the internal signifies the external.

The entire external, visible world with all its essence is a testimony or figure of the internal spiritual world. Everything that is in the interior, and however it exists in its operation, so it also has its character externally. Just as the spirit of every creature <term>creature: any created being, from minerals to humans</term> represents and reveals its internal form of birth through its body, so also does the eternal Essence. The Essence of all essences is a wrestling power—for the Kingdom of God stands in power, as does the external world—and it consists primarily of seven forms, where each one causes and creates the other. None is the first nor the last, but it is the eternal Bond. Therefore, God also ordained six days of work for man, and the seventh day is the perfection in which the six rest. It is the center <note>original: "Centrum"</note> toward which the six run with their desire; therefore God called it the Sabbath or day of rest. For therein the six forms of the working power rest. It is the Divine resonance in the power, or the kingdom of joy, in which the other forms all become manifest; for it is the contained

This same captured Word has revealed itself through the movement of all forms within this visible world, as if in a visible likeness, so that the spiritual Being stood revealed in a physical, comprehensible form. Just as the desire of the internal form has made itself external, so the internal now stands within the external. The internal holds the external before itself as a Mirror, wherein it beholds itself in the quality of the birth of all forms; the external is its Signature <note>The "Signature of All Things" is a central concept for the author, suggesting that the outward appearance of an object reveals its inner spiritual character</note>.

Thus, every single thing that has been born out of the internal also has its Signature. The highest form—that which is supreme in the power of the spirit's operation—marks the body <note>original: "corpus"</note> most significantly, and the other forms are attached to it. One sees this in all living creatures in the shape of the body, and in their customs and gestures. Likewise, it is seen in the resonance, voices, and languages, as well as in trees and herbs, in stones and metals; just as the struggle exists within the power of the spirit, so too does the figure of the body stand there. There are primarily seven forms in Nature, both in the eternal and the external; for the external ones proceed out of the eternal. The ancient Sages gave names to the seven Planets according to these seven forms of Nature; but they understood something quite different by them—not only the seven stars, but the sevenfold properties in the birthing of all beings. There is nothing in the essence of all essences that does not have these seven properties within itself: for they are the Wheel of the Center, the cause of the Sulfur <note>The combustible or "soul" principle in alchemy</note>, in which Mercury creates the boiling in the anguish-source <note>original: "Angstquall" — a technical term for the tension or "labor pains" of creation</note>.

The third form is called Mars; that is the fiery property within the impression of Saturn <note>Saturn represents the contracting, hardening force that creates matter.</note>, where the impression leads itself into great anguish, as if into a great hunger. in Mars, in the great anguish, to be free from that anguish; and it carries two forms within the desire of its own property: one fiery from Mars, and one watery from Jupiter. Understand this as a heavenly and an earthly desire. The heavenly arises from the heavenly [source] through the unification of the <term>Godhead</term><note>The essential nature of God as distinct from the created world</note> in Nature for its own self-revelation; and the earthly arises from the <term>Impression</term><note>The process of spiritual forces "pressing" or manifesting into physical form</note> of the darkness in Mars, as in the property of the fierce fire. Therefore, the essence of this desire stands in two things: in the Water from the origin of freedom, and in the <term>Sulphur</term><note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the active, combustible, and soul-principle of matter</note> from the origin of Nature according to the Impression. 

The outward likeness of the heavenly is Water and Oil; understand that according to the Sun <note>original: "Sol"</note> it is Water, and according to Jupiter it is Oil. And according to the hard Impression of Saturn, it is—according to the heavenly [part]—Copper according to Mars, and Gold according to the Sun. But according to the earthly Impression, following the property of darkness, it is Grit in the Sulphur—that is, Sand—according to the property of Mars, which is a cause of all stones. For stones are Sulphur [formed] out of the power of Saturn and Mars, in the property of Venus, according to the dark Impression—understand this as the earthly part. 

O, you dear Sages! If you knew what lies within Venus, you would not roof your houses so nobly; the powerful one often loses his life for the sake of the servant, and the servant covers the Master. <note>This likely refers to the alchemical belief that precious substances are hidden within common or "lowly" materials.</note>

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER IX.

In the desire of love, he is the decider of the resonance <note>original: "Hall," referring to the echo or reverberation of sound</note> in the spirit; he is understood as the one who determines the resonance, that is, the tone. Likewise, he governs all cries and languages, and everything that makes a sound. Through his power, everything is distinguished. Venus and Saturn carry his lutes for him, and he is the lute-player; he strikes upon Venus and Saturn, and Mars gives him the sound out of the fire. Thus Jupiter rejoices in the Sun <note>original: "Sol"</note>. Herein lies the mystery <note>original: "Mysterium"</note>, dear brothers: Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> creates understanding within Jupiter, for he distinguishes the senses so that they are fleeting <note>literally "flying"</note>; he gathers the infinity of the senses into his desire and makes them substantial. This he does in the Sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur," the active principle of growth and combustion</note>, and his essence is the manifold power of smell and taste; and Saturn adds his sharpness to it, so that it becomes a salt.

However, I am speaking here of the "power-salt" within vegetable life. Rock salt is made by Saturn in the water; he is a heavenly and earthly worker, and works in every form according to the property of that form, as it is written: *With the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse.* <note>A reference to Psalm 18:25-26, translated from the Latin Vulgate context</note> In the holy angels, the heavenly Mercury is holy and divine; and in the devils, he is the poison and the wrath of eternal nature according to the property of the dark impression <note>original: "Impression," the contraction or hardening of spiritual force into matter</note>; and so it continues through all things. Just as a thing possesses a specific property, so too is its Mercury.

For a weak fire in the body <term>corpus: the physical human body</term> which is exhausted by heat and inclines more toward cold than toward the poison of Mercury <note>original: "gifte deß Mercurii"; in this context, Mercury represents the volatile, transformative, and sometimes toxic principle of life forces</note>, where life is in danger, there belongs a cure with subtle heat, wherein Venus is strong and Mars is very gentle under the power of Venus. Jupiter must also not be strong there; otherwise, he makes Mars and Mercury too strong, so that he oppresses the weak life before it can refresh itself, and leads it into the Mercurial poison.

A herb of this aforementioned property does not grow high and is somewhat rough to the touch; the rougher it is, the stronger is Mars within it. Such a herb can be used more for external injuries than internally. That which is subtle belongs inside the body and drives outward; the more subtle it is, the closer it is to the life in the body, which the physician <term>Medicus: a doctor or healer</term> must recognize by its salt <note>In Paracelsian medicine, "Salt" is one of the three primary substances, representing the physical, crystalline, and stabilizing principle</note>. Every living creature, according to its kind within the above-mentioned property, is friendly and lovely if one deals with it in a friendly manner; but if one acts against it, then Mercury is awakened in the poisonous property, for Mars immediately rises up in the bitter property, and so anger wells forth. For the ground of all malice lies therein; but if it is not awakened, it does not become manifest—just as a great sickness lies within the body, but because it is hidden and not ignited, it is not manifest.

But if Venus is closest to Saturn in its property, and the Moon is not hindered by Mars, and Jupiter also moves in its own power, then everything is physical. The herbs become plain and soft to the touch, bearing white flowers—unless Mercury, through the power of the Sun, introduces a mixed color, such as half-red from Mars and bluish from Jupiter. There are three principal salts which may be used for curing, which belong to the vegetable life: namely Jupiter, Mars, and Mercury. These are the active life, in which the Sun is the true spirit that makes the salts effective.

If you say now: "I cannot; I am too greatly imprisoned," then I say: I also cannot. For it does not lie in my own willing, running, or racing, but it lies in God's mercy <note>original: "Gottes erbarmen"</note>. I will enter with my will out of the ignited poison-source, out of the evil Mercury in God's wrath, into His death; and with my corrupted will, I will die in and with Him in His death, and become a Nothingness in Him. Then He must become my life. For if my will is a Nothingness, then He is within me whatever He wills; then I no longer know myself, but only Him. But if He wills that I should be something, let Him make it so; if He wills it not, then I am dead in Him, and He lives in me as He wills. If I am then a Nothingness, I am at last in the essence from which my father Adam was created; for out of nothing God made everything. This Nothingness is the highest Good, for there is no turmoil <note>original: "turba," a Latin term used by Böhme to describe the spirit of destruction or disturbance</note> within it; thus, nothing can touch me.

I do not know myself: I am a thing and I know not what, for God knows what I am. But because Christ is born in me and lives in my "Nothingness" <term>Nichtsheit: the state of having no self-will, allowing God to act through the person</term>, Christ will surely—according to his promise in Paradise—tread upon the head of the serpent (which is my "I-ness") and kill the evil man within my "I-ness," so that He may live, who He Himself is.

# ON THE SIGNATURE
<note>original: "DE SIGNATURA" - likely referring to the "Signature of All Things," the idea that the inner essence of a thing is revealed in its outward form.</note>

Nature, as an enemy of the soul, stands opposed to the soul and always directs its poisonous imagination toward the soul to sift it. Because the wrath or fury of eternal nature is manifest in the outer man—which Adam awakened—this same fury is often awakened by the Devil and his servants, so that it operates and burns in the outer body. Then, the inner fire of love is extinguished in the outer man, just as a glowing iron is extinguished in water. This does not happen in the inner man immediately, but in the outer. This remains the case unless the outer man continues to lie in the water of sin, and the soul—which had previously surrendered itself into the "Nothing" <note>The "Nothing" or "Ungrund" refers to the absolute freedom of God before creation, a state of pure potentiality.</note> as into the freedom of the life of God—enters with its desire into the outer, sinful man. In such a case, the outer body must then have an external cure. Even if the inner man still lives in God, if the soul has imagined into the outer wrath so that the divine tincture <term>Tincture: a spiritual essence or "dye" that transforms and ennobles matter</term> is no longer in the outer man, then the outer Mercury <term>Mercury: in alchemy, the principle of movement, vitality, and the "spoken word" of nature</term>—as the expressed Word—must have a tincture from the outer expressed love and light. This is necessary unless the will-spirit of the soul enters entirely back into the inner, hidden man.

->Concerning the internal and external Cure of Man.<-

# ON THE SIGNATURE

What did God do with us while we lay sick in death? Did he cast away the created image—understand here the outer part, the outer corrupted human—and make an entirely new, foreign human? No, he did not do that. Even though he introduced divine properties into our humanity, he did not cast our humanity away because of it; rather, he led it into the process of <term>rebirth</term><note>German: Wiedergebuhrt; a central concept in Böhme's theology referring to the spiritual transformation of the soul.</note>. What did he do? He allowed the outer humanity—as the outer water, understand the essence of <term>Venus</term><note>German: Veneris weſenheit; in Böhme's alchemy, Venus represents the desire for love and the receptive, watery quality of the soul.</note>, which lay locked in the wrath of death—to be baptized with the water of eternal being and with the Holy Spirit. This was done so that the moldy decay of the outer essence, which had been locked in death, became glowing again, as if a fire had fallen into tinder. Afterward, he withdrew the outer food from the outer body and led it into the wilderness and let it hunger. Thus, the ignited spark from the fire of God had to imagine into God and eat <term>Manna</term><note>The "bread from heaven" provided to the Israelites; here used as a metaphor for spiritual sustenance.</note> from the divine essence for forty days. This is a prototype of what Israel did in the Wilderness of Sinai with their Manna. The essence of eternity had to overcome the essence of time. Therefore, it was called a temptation by the <term>Devil</term><note>German: Teuffel; the personification of the "wrath-fire" or the dark principle that tests the soul.</note>, for the Devil, as a prince in the wrath of God, tempted the outer humanity there and presented to it everything by which Adam had fallen and become disobedient to God.

# P R O C E S S
## In the Temptation.

Jesus was led by the Spirit into the wilderness; there the Devil approached him and tempted him.

When the soul of Christ hungered, the Devil spoke to Jesus: Unlock the center within the stone—that is, the impressed Mercury <note>original: "Mercurium"; in Boehme’s thought, Mercury represents the transformative, communicative power of the spirit within matter</note>—and make bread; eat of the essence of the soul's property. Why would you eat of Nothing, as if from the speaking Word? Eat of the expressed Word, as from the property of good and evil, and you shall be a master in both. That was also Adam’s bite, by which he ate death. Then Christ JESUS spoke: Man does not live by bread alone, but by every word that proceeds through the mouth of God. The properties of the Soul and the Body would have followed the Devil into God’s wrath, and made bread out of the locked-up <term>Mercury</term> <note>In Boehme’s alchemy, Mercury represents the restless, driving force of life and speech</note> and eaten it; thus the Will would have entered back into its own <term>Selfhood</term> <note>German: *Ichheit*; the state of being centered on the ego rather than God</note> and could not have been <term>transmuted</term> <note>transformed or purified, as base metal into gold</note>. ...to use their own self-property and depart from yieldedness <note>original: "gelassenheit" — a central mystical term meaning a state of surrender or letting go of the self-will to God</note>, entering into a selfhood within their own fiery will (as he did, and as Adam also did) when he entered with desire into his own power, into both evil and good. He wished to have his eyes opened to evil and good, as Moses writes concerning how the serpent had persuaded them of this. Now, at this point, that beautiful, adorned little beast <note>referring to the devil in his deceptive, angelic form</note> came again and attempted the same with the second Adam <note>Christ</note>. For God permitted this to him, since he claimed the fiery matrix <term>matrix: the womb or foundational source from which a specific quality or element is generated</term> had drawn him, and that he could not have withstood it; there he was to test it, for he had indeed been an angel just like the human soul which he had seduced. 

But the human property in body and soul within the person of Christ had once and for all cast itself into yieldedness, out of its "I-hood" <note>original: "Ichheit" — the ego or self-centered nature</note> and into God’s mercy. It stood still in yieldedness as if in the Divine will, and did not wish to fly of its own accord, nor do anything except what God did through it, and said to the Devil: *It is written, you shall not tempt the Lord your God.* This is as much as to say: a creature of God shall neither will nor do anything except what God wills and does through it. There shall be no other god who rules or wills but the One alone. The creature shall go and act as God’s Spirit of Will leads it; it shall be God’s instrument.

In this trial, Adam had not yet stood firm; for he departed from <term>yieldedness</term> <note>German: "Gelassenheit" — a central mystical concept meaning the state of having let go of self-will to be fully open to God</note> into a selfhood, into a self-will, and wanted to experience evil and good, love and anger, and to eat of evil and good. Here, dear human, was the state before the Tree of Temptation in Paradise; and that which the first Adam could not and would not do in obedience to God in yieldedness has now been fulfilled.

When the Devil saw that he could not succeed in making humanity depart from yieldedness out of God's will, he led humanity up to a high mountain and showed it all the riches of the world—everything that lives and moves in the <term>Expressed Word</term> <note>The "Expressed Word" refers to the physical creation as the outward manifestation of God's internal speaking</note>, all dominions and power in the outward nature, over which he calls himself a prince. # OF THE NATURE OF THINGS, CHAPTER X.

The desire and property of the **fire-world**, which revealed itself in human nature during the heavy **Fall of Adam**, led man—who was the image of an Angel—into a half-devilish mask and likeness. In this property and likeness of his will, within the expressed natural **Mercury** <note>In Boehme’s thought, "Mercury" refers to the "Life-Word" or the active, vibrating force that drives all growth and speech</note> or Word of Life, he cannot possess the Kingdom of God. Instead, he must be born anew with and in Christ, within his own Mercury and will, into the Love of God as the holy, speaking Mercury and Word of Life. 

This is so that a new obedience—a will entirely surrendered to God's Love—may proceed from his natural Mercury. This new will shall desire nothing and do nothing except what the will of the speaking Divine Mercury desires. He is as if dead in his **I-ness** and **selfhood** <note>original: "Ichheit vnd Selbstheit"; refers to the egoistic, self-centered will that separates man from God</note> within his own will, so that he may be the instrument of the great God, through which He makes and does what and how He wills. 

Then God is in him "all in all," his willing and his doing; he becomes a branch on the whole Tree, which draws its sap, power, and life from the Tree of God, growing, living, and bearing its fruit within Him. Then the Mercury of human life is a born or expressed fruit, which grows, sounds, and resonates upon the **Paradise-Tree** of God, and strikes the **Signature** <note>The "Signature" is the outward manifestation or "seal" of an inner spiritual essence</note> in the speaking Word of God strikes/

For it is written: Thou shalt worship God thy Lord, and Him only shalt thou serve." Then the Devil left Him, and the angels stepped forward and served Him.

Since God is the Creator of all beings, you must eat of God’s bread if you wish to <term>transmute</term> <note>To change from one form or substance into another, specifically from base to noble.</note> your body from earthly property into heavenly property. The mother of all beings is <term>Sulphur</term> <note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the soul and the principle of combustibility.</note>; <term>Mercurius</term> <note>Mercury represents the spirit and the principle of fluidity.</note> is her life; <term>Mars</term> is her feeling; <term>Venus</term> her love; <term>Jupiter</term> her understanding; <term>Luna</term> <note>The Moon.</note> her bodily being; <term>Saturnus</term> her husband. You must appease the husband with the wife, for the husband is wrathful; so give him his beloved bride into his arms. But see to it that the bride is a virgin, entirely modest and pure; for the woman’s seed shall bruise the head of the serpent (representing the husband’s wrath). The virgin must be in true love without any falsehood.

# OF MATTERS, CHAPTER X.

a virgin who has not yet touched a man in the anger of his masculinity. For the clear Deity, in pure love, likewise espouses itself with humanity; and when Mary said, "let it happen to me as you have said, for I am the Lord's handmaid," then humanity received the Deity, and likewise the Deity received humanity.

In the <term>Philosophical Work</term> <note>The "Great Work" or process of alchemical transmutation</note>, the clear Deity signifies the chaste Virgin. Humanity is <term>Mercury, Sulphur, and Salt</term> <note>The Tria Prima, or three primary substances of Paracelsian alchemy</note>, both heavenly and earthly. The heavenly part has faded and become as nothing; the dead part, dwelling in wrath, has awakened and lives for the Anger. In these properties of Anger, humanity was tempted both in Adam and also in Christ. Now you may ask: with what? With an equal opposition in the wrath; with such a Devil who possessed all these properties within himself, as a prince. The properties in the Sulphur were tempted with the <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> equality of the Sulphur. The temptation came in the Sulphur or out of the Sulphuric properties, and these forms were threefold. 

The first was in the <term>Impression</term> <note>The formative contraction of spiritual forces into matter</note>, which the Philosopher calls <term>Saturn</term> <note>Associated here with lead, gravity, and the restrictive material principle</note>. ### CHAPTER XI.

On the Process of Christ in his Suffering, Death, and Resurrection: On the Miracle of the Sixth Kingdom in the Mother of all Beings; how the <term>Consummatum est</term><note>Latin: "It is finished" (John 19:30)</note> was fulfilled, and how it runs to its end in the Philosophical Work in like manner.

We must consider it thus: we should know that the essence of this world, together with mankind, stands in two properties—namely, in Fire and Light, which is to say, in Love and Wrath. Now, the Fire is of two kinds, and the Light is also of two kinds: there is a cold fire from the <term>Impression</term><note>The contraction or "drawing-in" of spiritual forces into matter</note>, and a hot fire from the wheel of <term>Mercury</term><note>The principle of motion and transformation</note> within the <term>Sulphur</term><note>The combustible or soul-principle</note>. Likewise, there is a cold light from the cold fire, and a warming light from the hot fire. The cold light is false and the hot is good—not that it is false in its own essence, but only in its "impression" within the cold Sulphur.

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER XI.

From this, a death-terror arises within Love; but because no death can exist therein, Love sinks down within the fire-terror and yields itself up into its own desire. It leaves its essence so that it stands in its desire, according to its own property, within the death-terror. This is a poison to Death, and a pestilence to Hell <note>A reference to Hosea 13:14, "O death, I will be thy plagues; O grave, I will be thy destruction."</note>. In such a manner, the power of Death was taken away within humanity. For when Christ, in the terror of death, shed his heavenly blood and left it in death, the fury of God was compelled to receive the heavenly essence of Love into itself. There, the fire-desire within ignited humanity was <term>transmuted</term> <note>original: "transmutiret"; a term from alchemy meaning the changing of one substance into another, here used for the spiritual change from wrath to love</note> into a love-desire, and out of the anguish of death, a joy and strength of Divine power was born.

Christ overcame the fury of death within the human property, and [overcame] the Wrath of the Father into a Love within the human property.

God desired to transmute humanity back into a Divine, heavenly property after it had become earthly and had awakened the poisonous Mercury within the property of love—which had swallowed the love and transmuted it into itself. # OF THINGS, CHAPTER XI.

When Christ walked upon the Earth, the humanity derived from Mary’s nature was not almighty; rather, it was the humanity derived from God. These were set against one another in two principles <note>The author uses "principles" to describe the fundamental spiritual forces of Light/Love and Darkness/Wrath</note>, yet they were not locked away from each other, but both were manifest within one another: Love against Wrath, and Wrath against Love. Now, it was a matter of wrestling together; from this also came the temptation of Christ. And as the Divine World triumphed, great wonders followed through the external human world. 

But all of this could not complete the work; there had to be a greater earnestness. The human quality, as the "spoken Word" <note>original: "ausgesprochene Wort" – referring to the manifestation of God's thought into physical form</note>, was in its selfhood still active within the movable wrath. The human <term>Sulphur</term> <note>In theosophical alchemy, Sulphur represents the internal fire or soul-spirit that drives growth and desire</note> had to be transmuted into the heavenly—into the heavenly part. And at this, the human selfhood was terrified, acting as the "spoken" <term>Mercury</term> <note>Mercury here refers to the "Mercurius," the principle of movement, sound, and the vital force that mediates between spirit and matter</note>. 

This occurred on the Mount of Olives, where the heavenly world in Love wrestled with the Wrath in the human world—that is, with the Selfhood—so that the person of Christ sweated bloody sweat. There, one trembled before the other: Love trembled before the smoking death, into which it should and must entirely surrender itself with the Divine Essence <note>original: "Wesenheit"</note> and allow the Wrath to be swallowed up within itself; and the Wrath trembled before its own death, in which it was to lose its power within Love. Thus the entire person of Christ spoke: *Father, if it is possible* <note>original Latin/German: "Vatter/ ists möglich" – quoting Christ's prayer in Gethsemane</note>

Therefore, the second Adam, Christ, had to take all of this upon himself and enter into that same mockery. When Jesus broke death within humanity and took away the <term>Selfhood</term> <note>The ego or selfish will that separates the soul from God</note>, he did not cast away the human properties in which death and the wrath of God resided. Instead, he truly took them up for the first time. Understand this: He truly took the outer kingdom into the inner. For the outer kingdom is a wonder born out of the eternal Wisdom in the speaking Word, and spoken into a form as a revelation of the Deity in love and wrath, in good and evil. Thus, JESUS did not want the outer image of these wonders—made in the likeness of God—to perish. Rather, the fierceness <note>original: "grim." Refers to the harsh, consuming quality of fire or anger.</note> which had overwhelmed love within man was to be forgiven. That is, it was to be surrendered into the "Nothing," into the Freedom, so that it would no longer be manifest in its own self-property. It was to become a servant, acting only as a cause for the fiery love and the kingdom of joy. Nothing in man was to perish, for God created him in His own image.

Therefore, the <term>Philosopher</term> <note>Here meaning the Alchemist or seeker of natural wisdom</note> should note: when the three murderers—namely <term>Saturn, Mars, and Mercury</term> <note>In alchemy, these represent Lead/Contraction, Iron/Conflict, and Quicksilver/Motion</note>—are drowned in the rose-colored blood of the <term>Lion</term> <note>The "Lion" often symbolizes Gold or the potent solar tincture in its perfected state</note>, they do not perish. Instead, they are forgiven. # ON THE SIGNATURE

<margin>The duty of true teachers</margin>
The disciples and teachers of Christ should act and take care of poor Christendom <note>original: "Christenheit"</note> as sons with great humility toward their mother. They should serve her with earnestness and diligence, tending to her in a fine, modest, and humble manner, guiding and comforting her with the Spirit of Christ. They should not act like the priests of Babel <note>A common Reformation-era term for the corrupt or worldly Church establishment</note>, who, like fat and wealthy lords, ride over her and wish to be masters over the mother. These seek only honors and fat bellies in luxury, living in strife. All of these together, by whatever name they may be called, are not "Johannites" <note>Followers of the spirit of the Apostle John, often associated with divine love and mystical insight</note>. Instead, they are the poisonous, mercurial Pharisees <note>term: Mercurialischen Phariseer; those driven by the restless, unstable, and prideful aspects of the "Mercury" spirit without the light of God</note>, in whom there is nothing but anxiety, martyrdom, and torment. In them, one quality always plagues and hostiles the other, deeming it false.

And yet, they all spring from a single root and all possess one will, only one color does not shine like the other. For Saturn is not as Jupiter; Jupiter is not as Mars; Mars is not as the fire-spirit; and the fire-spirit is not as the light of the Sun; and the Sun is not as Venus with her gentle water-source; and Venus is not as Mercury with his sound, for she is gentle and still, while Mercury rings and sounds; likewise, Mercury is not as the Moon <note>original: "Luna"</note>, which, as a simple body, gives a body to all the others for their manifestation. Each is indeed different from the other, and they do not share one single quality <note>term: Eigenschafft; a fundamental characteristic or "signature" of a being's nature</note> or will, and they are—

It happens in the same way in the **philosophical work** <note>The "philosophical work" refers to Alchemy, specifically the process of transmuting base matter into gold, which Boehme uses as a metaphor for spiritual rebirth</note>. For in the beginning, Paradise was manifest as the **Universal** <note>The "Universal" or "tincture" that heals all things</note>, and Love shone through death or anger. It must become so again: that Venus becomes the "eye" or the vision within the wrath. Then, out of Saturn, Mars, and Mercury, a Jupiter is formed; Mars becomes the Sun, and Saturn becomes the Moon. Thus Mars shines with the Sun out of Saturn into the Moon from the eye of Venus. And all seven <note>The seven planetary spirits or properties of nature</note> are but one; then the strife has an end, and all is accomplished, until the resurrection of the body.

# ON THE SIGNATURE

And so we must all follow Him upon the path He has opened; no one may behold God unless God first becomes human within them <note>original: "Gott Mensch"; a central concept in Boehme’s theology where the divine must be birthed in the soul</note>. This occurs within the desire of faith. Then, the corrupted will—which is seized by death and the wrath of God, and which flourishes in earthly essence and brings forth fruit unto death—must completely die away and fall into free resignation <term>Gelassenheit: a state of yieldedness or letting go of the self-will to allow God to work</term> within God’s will and mercy. Then the self-will is with and in Christ at the end of Nature, within the great Mystery of God <note>original: "Mysterio Gottes"</note>, as if in God’s hands. God’s hands are the eternal desire, or the eternal will, which is immutable. Thus the will of the creaturely selfhood dies; it goes entirely into Nothingness, so that it no longer lives for itself, but God lives within it.

And it happens exactly this way in the Philosophical Work <note>The "Philosophical Work" refers to the alchemical process of transmuting base matter into gold, used here as a metaphor for spiritual perfection</note>. When the artist has previously seen great wonders, which the creaturely and natural will has wrought through the power of Venus <note>In alchemy, Venus represents the copper principle or the force of love and desire in nature</note>, and he believes he is close to the goal, it is then that Nature first dies for him in his work. It becomes for him a dark night; all forms, properties, and powers must surrender themselves from their center <note>original: "Centro"</note> and fall at the end of Nature. Everything gives itself up freely, as a dead being; there is no more accomplishment there; everything divides itself in the "Crown" into a thousand numbers. Then it is once again in the Mystery...

<meta>catchword: Mystery</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that spiritual union with God requires the total death of the self-will, a process he parallels with the "dark night" or death of nature encountered by an alchemist in the Great Work.</summary>

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER XI.

By the Mystery, at the end of Nature / as it was before it entered into the creature / understand the essential Desire / as the expressed Mercury <note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of volatility and the "spirit" of a substance.</note> / which must likewise return to the end of its selfhood / and surrender itself into the speaking Word. # ON THE SIGNATURE

The <term>Regiment</term><note>The governing power or internal rule of the soul</note> which ruled in man—belonging to the self-will and the individual powers of the creaturely selfhood, wherein man became disobedient to God—He gave entirely into the Father’s hands, as into the end of Nature, into the greatest <term>Mysterium</term><note>The hidden divine mystery or foundational essence</note> of the Father. This was not so that it should be dead, but so that God’s Spirit alone should be the life within it; that in Christ’s person there should be the Divine Government; that the eternal Father might rule and judge in His own image with His eternal Spirit. For this reason, God has decreed to hold the Last Judgment through this **JESUS**. Thus, it is not the creature of Christ alone who acts, but God in His own image through the creature, in the government of His eternal Spirit of all three <term>principles</term><note>The three realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light world, and the outward world</note>, which is the life and government of all being, in every thing according to its own property. 

And so understand us: when Christ died on the <term>Cross</term><note>original: "Creutze"</note>, the name **JESUS** did not die with Him—for it was He who broke death and, with Love, <term>tinctured</term><note>An alchemical term meaning to permeate, transform, or ennoble a substance</note> the expressed Word (as the form of the Deity, as the formed Word, as the soul). No, that cannot be; eternity does not die. Only that which is expressed—which stands again in the desire of speaking, as in the <term>Fiat</term><note>Latin: "Let it be"; the creative command of God</note>—transforms itself in its own speaking, as in its own desire, and leads its own sound into another form.

<summary>This page explores how Christ surrendered the human self-will to the Father to allow the Divine Spirit to rule, explaining that while the physical form died on the cross, the eternal Word and the name of Jesus remain as the transformative power over all nature.</summary>

# OF THE NATURE OF THINGS, CHAPTER XII.

->Form and Quality<-

as the speaking Word <note>original: "sprechende Wort"; a reference to the creative breath of God</note> has spoken it into a form and quality, and placed it into a form, shape, and will through the *word of creation* <note>original: "verbo fiat"; the Latin command "let there be"</note>. This is exactly what Lucifer with his royal throne, and also Adam, have done, when they both departed from **resignation** <note>original: "Gelassenheit"; the mystical state of letting go of the self</note> and entered into **selfhood**. The instrument wished to be the Master. The outward, active, and feeling life, in which the wrath of God burned, died away entirely—not that it became a "nothing," but rather it fell into the **Nothing**, as if into God's will, into God's working and feeling. It fell away entirely from the will of the outer world, to which "evil and good" belong, so that it no longer lived for the world or for the constellations in the seething of the four elements. Instead, it lived for the nature of the Eternal Father in the seething of the pure Divine Element; the life of the outer world died. 

Thus, the true human life immediately fell back into the place from which Adam had led it away, namely into **Paradise**. Regarding this, Christ said to the thief <note>original: "Schecher"; the criminal on the cross</note>: *Today you will be with me in Paradise.* It fell into Adam's dying—when he died to Paradise—and it sprouted green within Adam's dying as a new creature out of the old, just as the stalk grows out of the grain of corn. And this occurred in the power and might of the **speaking Word**.

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER XII.

As I penetrate out of my selfhood into Him, I die daily to my selfhood, until I shall one day reach the goal of my selfhood, and my selfhood—with the earthly will and desire of the self—shall completely die away. ### A Brief Summary of the Philosophical Work

Our meaning might appear quite difficult to the reader, in that we proceed at such length and indicate Christ therein; no one should wonder at this. We seek neither gold nor temporal goods thereby, nor do we drive man into vain curiosity. We speak only with the children whom God has chosen for this purpose; for the time is born when that which was lost shall be found again: and not only the <term>universal</term><note>original: "universal"; the universal medicine or elixir capable of perfecting both metals and the human body</note> for the body of this world, but also that for the soul.

The process is very short in both cases, and is of only one quality; it is concealed thus: The Tree is divided into <term>seven forms</term><note>original: "sieben Gestalten"; in Boehme’s cosmology, these are the seven source-spirits or properties of nature</note> (understand this as the Life). Now, the <term>curse of God</term><note>original: "fluch Gottes"; the disharmony introduced by the Fall of Man</note> has entered into the seven forms; thus they are in conflict and opposition, and each form sickens the other, and they may never become one. They all seven go into death and die away from their own will. Now, this cannot happen unless a Death comes into them which breaks the will of them all, and is a Death unto them,

<summary>The author explains that his alchemical writings aim for spiritual salvation rather than material gold, describing how the seven conflicting forms of nature must undergo a "death" of the self-will to be restored.</summary>
<keywords>Apocalypse, Philosophical Work, Universal Medicine, Seven Forms of Nature, Curse of God, Self-Will, Spiritual Alchemy</keywords>

# ON THE SIGNATURE

Just as the Divinity in Christ was a death to the human selfhood, and yet had come to life for the seven forms <note>The "seven forms" or "seven properties" of nature are a central concept in Böhme’s theosophy, representing the forces that constitute all existence.</note> in human life: so it is here also. The human will in Christ was transformed into the eternal Sun, as if into a state of yieldedness <note>original: "Gelassenheit" — a key mystical term meaning total surrender of the self-will to God</note> in God. In the same way, in the philosophical works, all forms must transform themselves into one, namely into *Sol* <note>Latin for "Sun," representing gold in alchemy and divine light in theology</note>. Out of seven, one must be made, and yet it remains in seven, but in one single desire, where each form desires the other in love; then there is no more conflict. Therefore, let the <term>Artist</term> <note>The alchemist or spiritual seeker practicing the "Great Work."</note> only consider how he may give death to Death with the pure Life, and how he may awaken the dead and faded Life—which is heavenly, yet stands captive and hidden under the curse of God—so that the fire-soul may take it into itself again. If he can only bring it that far, then it has its own making within itself.

When the Virgin <note>Representing the "Sophia" or Divine Wisdom.</note> again receives her Bridegroom, who had become unfaithful to her, then he is prepared for it; otherwise, there is no way at all, and everything else is in vain. There is also no other possibility; the heavenly image according to God’s likeness could not be restored in man after the fire-soul entered into its own selfhood, unless the Spirit of God then led itself into the faded image, as into the heavenly essence.

<summary>Böhme explains the alchemical and spiritual necessity of unifying the seven properties of nature into a single "Solar" desire of love, mirroring Christ's victory over the self-will. He emphasizes that the "Artist" must awaken the hidden heavenly life within the soul to achieve restoration.</summary>
<keywords>Christ, selfhood, seven forms of nature, Gelassenheit, Sol, alchemy, fire-soul, Divine Virgin, Sophia, essence</keywords>

And he should surrender himself, with that same image awakened within him, into the fire of the soul, as if into the fury of death, and would become a death unto death—namely, to the fierce wrath of God—so that he might be drowned in love within the blood of the heavenly essence. In this way, the faded essence receives the fire-terror <note>original: "Fewerschrack" — a sudden, transformative spiritual shock or flash of divine fire.</note> back into itself and constrains itself entirely into the fire-life. Then the fire must burn in love and gentleness and abandon its "right" <note>Its natural tendency toward harshness or destruction.</note> in the center <note>original: "Centro" — the spiritual core or point of origin.</note>. Just as the light shines out of the fire, in this way and no other does the heavenly essence receive its life. # OF THE NATURE OF THINGS, CHAPTER XII.

The fire-soul <note>original: "Feuerseele"; in Boehme's thought, this represents the primal, energetic core of human life</note> is revealed, and out of seven wills it makes only one; and yet seven remain, but the enmity ceases. Thus, this is a universal <note>a "universal" refers to a panacea or a fundamental spiritual truth that applies to all levels of existence</note>, which also transmutes the opposition of all sickness in the human body into one will, so that the raging and stinging—as the seven forms of life <note>the "seven forms" or "seven properties" are Boehme's foundational categories of existence, corresponding to both planetary forces and psychological states</note>—become united in their enmity. Then the hunger of the sickness ceases. The process for the universal is as was mentioned before: it is not my intention to provide a clearer indication; it is clear enough. Whoever does not wish to seek a new man, born in God, through this process, and to prepare himself for it, let him leave my writings in peace.

I have written nothing for that kind of seeker, nor can he entirely grasp our meaning. No matter how much he practices, unless he enters into the yieldedness in Christ <note>original: "Gelassenheit in Christo"; a central mystical concept meaning total surrender of the self-will to God</note>, where he may grasp the spirit of the universal; otherwise, it is all in vain. We wish to warn the over-curious <note>original: "Vorwitz"; implying a superficial or meddling intellectual curiosity</note> that he should not be deceived; he will accomplish nothing at all on this path unless he enters into it himself. Then it will be revealed to him without much searching, for the way is childlike.

### CHAPTER XIII.

### Concerning the Antipathy of Spirit and Body, and Concerning its Cure and Healing.

Every body is in itself a mute and, as it were, a dead thing or being: It is only a manifestation of the spirit which is within the body. The spirit signs itself <note>The "signature" is the outward mark of an inner spiritual reality</note> with the body; what the spirit is in itself in its incomprehensible working, that the body is in the comprehensible and visible. There is always one form among the seven forms of Nature <term>Seven Forms of Nature: Boehme's system of the fundamental forces of reality, such as contraction, expansion, and rotation</term> that is the highest; the others cling to it and provide their contributing signs, according to how strong each one is in its essence. And as the forms stand in their order in every thing, so they figure the body—every thing and creature in its own kind. This is the manifestation of Divine Wisdom in the spoken Word out of Love and Wrath <note>original: "Liebe vnd Zorn"</note>. No thing exists that does not have its soul within it according to its property, and the soul is a seed for another body. Everything that lives and grows has its seed within itself; God has grasped all things in His Word and spoken them out into a form, just as the Will has grasped itself in Desire. That which is spoken is a model of the speaker, and has the speaking within itself again; that same speaking is a

<summary>Boehme explains that the physical body is a visible manifestation of the internal spirit, shaped by the seven forms of nature and the creative Word of God. He posits that all living things contain a "soul" or seed that reflects the divine balance of love and wrath.</summary>
<keywords>signature, spirit and body, seven forms of nature, divine wisdom, the Word, macrocosm and microcosm, spiritual essence</keywords>

### OF THE NATURE OF THINGS, CHAPTER XIII.

The Seed <note>original: "Sahme"; here referring to the creative potential or germ of being</note> leads to another image after the first: For both are active, namely the speaking and the spoken. The speaking exists within itself as if in eternity, and the spoken also exists within itself as if in time. The speaking is the Master, and the spoken is the instrument. The speaking creates the nature of eternity, and the spoken creates the nature of time. Each creates within its own conception two properties, such as Light and Darkness; therein stands the Element of all beings, which in the spoken world divides into four elements, but in the speaking world is only one. 

The Element is in itself neither hot nor cold, nor dry or wet, but is a delight, like a desiring will, wherein the Divine Wisdom <note>original: "Göttliche Weißheit"; often personified as Sophia in this mystical tradition</note> creates the colors of distinction, all according to the property of the desire. In this there is neither number nor end; only in the four elements is there a number and an end, for they took a beginning when they became distinct through the speaking, and they composed themselves into a model of a time. This runs within itself like a clockwork: it forms, shapes, and breaks. This clockwork stands in seven forms or properties (as mentioned previously), which create within themselves a threefold spirit: namely a growing, a feeling, and a rational spirit.

<summary>This page describes the metaphysical distinction between the eternal "speaking" Word and the temporal "spoken" world, explaining how the unified divine element divides into the four physical elements and a clockwork-like temporal reality.</summary>
<keywords>Eternity vs Time, The Word, Four Elements, Divine Wisdom, Clockwork of Nature, Threefold Spirit, Seven Properties</keywords>

--- Page 299 ---
<meta>The previous page likely discussed the foundational principles of the Signature of All Things; this page transitions from the cosmic origins of life to the practical necessity of healing the human condition.</meta>

# ON THE SIGNATURE

That which merely grows exists within the four elements; that which feels exists within the seven forms of nature; and that which is rational exists within the stars. But true understanding comes from God alone, for it originates from eternal nature. All life that has its goal within the "spoken word" <note>original: "ausgeprochenen Wort"; referring to the Divine Word or Logos through which the world was created</note> exists within Salt, Sulphur, and Mercury <note>The *Tria Prima* or three primary substances of alchemy representing body, soul, and spirit</note>. For within these three reside the seven properties of all life in this world, including the spirit of growth, of feeling, and of reason.

Sulphur is the mother of all spirituality and physicality; Mercury holds the governance therein; and Salt is the house of their dwelling, which Mercury himself builds within the Sulphur. Reason arises in the oil of the Sulphur, wherein the stars <term>the stars: the "astrum" or celestial influences that shape human temperament</term> bestow their desire as the essence of their property. From this, the senses and thoughts then arise. However, true understanding stems from the oil of the Element—that is, in the free delight of the "speaking Mercury" <note>The divine communicative principle</note>.

Since it is now necessary for us poor children of Eve <note>A traditional reference to humanity in its fallen or suffering state</note> to know the source of our sickness and the aversions of life—to know what it is within us that makes us our own enemy, plaguing and agonizing itself—it is even more necessary for us to know the Cure <note>original: "Cur"</note>. By this, we may heal ourselves in our selfhood <note>original: "selbstheit"; the state of individual ego or being</note> and lead ourselves into the ultimate state of rest. Thus, we wish to outline this path, should anyone desire to walk upon it.

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER XIII.

Tester and Knower of all properties; for he is born through all, and has proceeded from all: He is as a Nothing, and yet has everything; he passes through heat and cold, and neither seizes him, as we see that the life of the creature dwells in heat and cold, and is the true life, yet is neither hot nor cold.

Thus you should now understand us correctly: in eternity this birth is spiritual, but in time it is also material; for I cannot say of God that he is darkness and fire, much less air, water, or earth; but in his eternal desire he has thus, with time, in the <term>place of this world</term><note>original: "loco dieser Welt"</note> composed himself into such an essence, which he has grasped in the <term>speaking Mercury</term><note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of volatility and the "word" or "spirit" that mediates between the soul and body.</note> according to the properties of the will, and with the spoken word brought into such a formation, according to the properties of desire in eternal nature, as in the <term>let it be done by the word</term><note>original Latin: "verbo fiat." A reference to the creative command in Genesis.</note>.

Now the spoken word is understood as the property of eternal nature in <term>Sulphur</term><note>In the Paracelsian "tria prima," Sulphur represents the soul, the combustible principle, and the expansive force of life.</note>, for therein is the sevenfold <term>wheel of birth</term><note>A central concept in Boehme's theosophy, referring to the seven qualities or "fountains" of nature that drive the process of manifestation.</note>, which in the spirit—as in the first grasping toward nature—is a constellation, and divides itself out of the constellation in its own birth into 7 properties.

That is the Chaos, or the first Star-cluster, wherein stands the eternal Nature, which introduces itself into seven forms through the Desire for Nature, as previously mentioned and revealed. Thus, the Chaos is the eternal hidden Wisdom of God; and through the Desire in the Mercurial wheel, the Element is conceived.

He, however, went out from the Free Longing of God, out of that surrender, and into a self-will, which he drew from the Center toward Nature, from which the pain and torment originate.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the alchemical and spiritual nature of matter, specifically focusing on the "Mercurial Wheel" and the consequences of the divine curse on the earth.</meta>

# OF THINGS, CHAPTER XIII.

The earth has been cursed; for from the **Mercurial Wheel** <note>The "Mercurial Wheel" refers to the dynamic, rotating force of nature and consciousness in Böhme’s cosmology</note> its "good" was withdrawn. This good is the **desire of love**, which originates in Eternal Freedom and reveals itself through this Mercurial Wheel by means of cold and heat, and which goes forth through the **fire** and creates a shine of light. In its place, the curse was introduced, which is a fleeing of the desire of love. Thus, this **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"; here representing the vital, communicative spirit in all matter</note> now stands as a life within the **Sulphur** <note>original: "Sulphur"; representing the formative, substantial principle or "mother" of a substance</note> of its mother, currently under the curse, existing in the anguish of heat and cold. 

In its "terror" or its **salt-nitrous** <note>original: "Salnitrischen"; referring to a volatile, explosive spiritual energy</note> seething, it continually produces **salt** according to the specific quality of the place and body in which it is ignited. These salts are merely the "taste" within the seven properties. If it happens that the Mercury is ignited too strongly in the cold, it creates a cold, hard, impressing salt within the salt-nitrous terror of its mother, the Sulphur. From this, **melancholy**, darkness, and sadness arise in the life of the fire. For whatever kind of salt is in a thing, such a luster of fire and such a shine of life from the fire is also within it. 

But if Mercury is ignited in excessive heat, it burns away the essence of the cold and creates raging and stinging, according to the "impression" <note>The process by which spiritual forces compress into material form</note> and the property of the "sting," from which great heat arises within the Sulphur.

<summary>Böhme explains how the divine curse altered the "Mercurial Wheel" of nature, leading to the creation of different types of salts and emotional states like melancholy through the imbalance of heat and cold.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, Sulphur, Salt, Salt-nitre, Melancholy, Alchemy, Four Temperaments, Spiritual Fire, The Curse</keywords>

And when the water is dried up and consumed, then the hunger or sting of desire has no food with which it may still its fierce hunger. Thus it rages and tears at the salt, as is the nature of poison, from which the pain in the flesh arises. However, if it can regain its likeness—in the same quality as it stands in the center of its mother, namely in the <term>Sulphur</term><note>In Böhme’s alchemy, Sulphur represents the "burning oil" or the soul's light-substance.</note>—understand this as how she first bore it in the beginning, when it first came to natural life in the two <term>Tinctures</term><note>The spiritual essences or "dyes" of life.</note> of the man and the woman (understand this in the child where its life was first ignited), then it is released from all anguish. It steps back into the balance of heat and cold. And although it is true that in many people this strife comes along from the mother’s womb, the conflict only truly arises after the beginning of life. At the start of life, life enters into its highest joy, for the gates of the three <term>principles</term><note>The three realms: the dark fire-world, the light love-world, and the outer physical world.</note> are opened in equal harmony; but the strife begins immediately regarding the victory between darkness and light.

Thus we must now consider what should be done for <term>Mercury</term><note>The "Mercury" here refers to the vital, moving spirit or "workman" within the body that regulates health.</note> if it has become ignited in heat or cold, whereby it awakens sickness and pain. It would indeed be good if one had the right cure, but unfortunately, it chooses to remain very hidden because of the **curse**. <note>original: "Fluchs" — referring to the biblical Fall of Man which Böhme believes obscured true natural knowledge.</note>

There would be no being <note>original: "Wesen" — refers to both essence and substance</note>, neither body nor spirit, nor any revelation of the eternity of the Abyss <note>original: "vngrund" — Jacob Böhme’s term for the groundless, undifferentiated divine nothingness from which all things emerge</note>, if this were not so. But since the bitter sting cannot rise above itself, and the hardness cannot hold or enclose it, they fall into a turning or a breaking-through, like a wheel that revolves within itself as a terrifying being. In this state, the two properties are recognized as only one, yet each remains unchanged within itself. Between them, they give birth to the third property: the great Anguish. From this anguish, the Will—meaning the first Will toward Nature—desires to go out again into Freedom, into the Nothing, and into eternal Rest. For it has now found itself here and revealed itself, yet there is no separating or retreating. 

This form of anguish is the mother of Sulphur <term>Sulphur: in Böhme's alchemy, this represents the "burning" or "active" principle of the soul's desire</term>. The sting makes it painful, and the hardness compresses it so that it is like a dying torment, yet it is the true origin of life. It contains two properties within itself: according to the impression or desire, it is dark and hard; but according to the desire of the Will—which wishes to be free from the anguish and enters back into Freedom—it is spiritual and light. The sting breaks its composed being, which the harsh desire grasps, so that its being is hard and brittle, and entirely fleeting like a flash of lightning; and such

And do understand us correctly: the Freedom does not wish to be a Nothing; for this reason, the longing of the Freedom introduces itself into Nature and Essence, because it desires to be manifest in power, wonders, and being.

For in the Fire, it consumes the dark essence and goes forth out of the Fire with the **Spiritual properties**—derived from the anguish of the Impression—into the **Light**. Rightly so, I will now speak of the external kingdom, as of the third principle or beginning; for in this world, light and darkness are also within one another, just as in eternity. # OF THINGS, CHAPTER XIV.

In the <term>mortification</term><note>original: "ertödtung" — the chemical process of "killing" or neutralizing a substance's active state</note> of the fire, everything becomes Water, as if entering a living kingdom of joy according to the Light. Understand us further: in the fire-crack of <term>Saltpeter</term><note>original: "Salniter" — seen by Böhme as a volatile, celestial force within matter</note> arise the various salts and powers. This same "crack" occurs in the ignition of the fire, and also impresses itself in the mortification of the fire arising from the origin of the Water.
